=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integers N, M and three integer sequences of length M: X = (X_1, X_2, \ldots, X_M), Y = (Y_1, Y_2, \ldots, Y_M), and Z = (Z_1, Z_2, \ldots, Z_M). It is guaranteed that all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N, inclusive.
We call a length-N sequence of non-negative integers A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N) a good sequence if and only if it satisfies the following condition:

- For every integer i with 1 \le i \le M, the XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i.

Determine whether a good sequence A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) exists, and if it exists, find one good sequence that minimizes the sum of its elements \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N A_i.

Notes on XOR

For non-negative integers A and B, their XOR A \oplus B is defined as follows:


- In the binary representation of A \oplus B, the digit in the place corresponding to 2^k \,(k \ge 0) is 1 if and only if exactly one of the digits in the same place of A and B is 1; otherwise, it is 0.


For example, 3 \oplus 5 = 6 (in binary: 011 \oplus 101 = 110).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
X_1 Y_1 Z_1
X_2 Y_2 Z_2
\vdots
X_M Y_M Z_M

Output

If no good sequence exists, print -1.
If a good sequence exists, print one good sequence that minimizes the sum of its elements, separated by spaces.
If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted.

Constraints


- 1 \le N \le 2\times 10^5
- 0 \le M \le 10^5
- 1 \le X_i, Y_i \le N
- 0 \le Z_i \le 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
1 3 4
1 2 3

Sample Output 1

0 3 4

A=(0,3,4) is a good sequence because A_1 \oplus A_2 = 3 and A_1 \oplus A_3 = 4.
Other good sequences include A=(1,2,5) and A=(7,4,3), but A=(0,3,4) has the smallest sum among all good sequences.

Sample Input 2

3 3
1 3 4
1 2 3
2 3 5

Sample Output 2

-1

No good sequence exists, so print -1.

Sample Input 3

5 8
4 2 4
2 3 11
3 4 15
4 5 6
3 2 11
3 3 0
3 1 9
3 4 15

Sample Output 3

0 2 9 6 0

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,M=map(int,input().split())
    adj=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(M):
        x,y,z=map(int,input().split())
        adj[x].append((y,z))
        if x!=y: # self-loop no need duplicate? If add only x!=y, self-loop z!=0 would not be checked unless process? If x==y and we skip, we'd miss inconsistency. So don't skip; add both? For self-loop, adding once is enough. If x==y, adj[x].append((x,z)). If we also add reverse? For x!=y, need add both directions because undirected constraint. For x==y, adding twice unnecessary but okay. So:
        adj[x].append((y,z))
        if x != y:
            adj[y].append((x,z))
        # if x==y, only one self-loop. Good.
    base=[-1]*(N+1)
    ans=[0]*(N+1)
    K=30
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        if base[i] != -1: continue
        base[i]=0
        stack=[i]
        comp=[]
        while stack:
            u=stack.pop()
            comp.append(u)
            bu=base[u]
            for v,z in adj[u]:
                expected=bu^z
                if base[v] == -1:
                    base[v]=expected
                    stack.append(v)
                elif base[v] != expected:
                    print(-1); return
        counts=[0]*K
        for u in comp:
            b=base[u]
            # count bits
            # for k in range(K): if b>>k &1: counts[k]+=1
            # Could optimize by while b: but K small.
            for k in range(K):
                if (b >> k) & 1:
                    counts[k]+=1
        c=0
        ncomp=len(comp)
        for k in range(K):
            if counts[k] * 2 > ncomp: # ones > zeros
                c |= 1<<k
        for u in comp:
            ans[u]=base[u]^c
    print(' '.join(map(str, ans[1:])))

Draft code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N_M = input().split()
    if not N_M: return
    N = int(N_M[0]); M = int(N_M[1])
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(M):
        x,y,z = map(int, input().split())
        adj[x].append((y,z))
        if x != y:
            adj[y].append((x,z))
    base = [-1]*(N+1)
    ans = [0]*(N+1)
    K = 30
    for s in range(1,N+1):
        if base[s] != -1:
            continue
        base[s] = 0
        stack = [s]
        comp = []
        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            comp.append(u)
            bu = base[u]
            for v,z in adj[u]:
                nb = bu ^ z
                if base[v] == -1:
                    base[v] = nb
                    stack.append(v)
                elif base[v] != nb:
                    print(-1)
                    return
        cnt = [0]*K
        for u in comp:
            b = base[u]
            # maybe use while b:
            # for k in range(K):
            #    cnt[k] += (b >> k) & 1
            k=0
            while b:
                if b & 1:
                    cnt[k] += 1
                b >>= 1
                k += 1
            # But if b has bit beyond K? no. If b=0 no loop.
        c = 0
        half? Need compare counts[k] > len(comp)//2? Since choose 1 if ones > zeros. zeros = n - ones. ones > n - ones iff 2*ones > n. Use if cnt[k] * 2 > ncomp: c |= 1<<k. If tie choose 0. Good.
        ncomp = len(comp)
        for k in range(K):
            if cnt[k] * 2 > ncomp:
                c |= 1 << k
        for u in comp:
            ans[u] = base[u] ^ c
    sys.stdout.write(' '.join(map(str, ans[1:])))

Now, let's think about if there is a more subtle mathematical issue: The sum of elements is over integers, and XOR is bitwise. We minimized each bit's contribution independently. But the numeric sum of numbers is not simply sum of bit contributions if there are carries? Wait, let's revisit this because it's crucial. The value of an integer X is Σ_k bit_k(X) * 2^k. This is true by definition of binary representation. There is no "carry" in the value; carries appear when adding two numbers in binary, but the sum of two numbers equals sum of their bit contributions because addition is linear over powers of two. For multiple numbers, sum_i X_i = sum_i Σ_k bit_k(X_i) 2^k = Σ_k 2^k Σ_i bit_k(X_i). This is exact. So yes.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())

    adj = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    for _ in range(M):
        x, y, z = map(int, input().split())
        adj[x].append((y, z))
        if x != y:
            adj[y].append((x, z))

    K = 30
    base = [-1] * (N + 1)
    ans = [0] * (N + 1)

    for s in range(1, N + 1):
        if base[s] != -1:
            continue

        base[s] = 0

        # Isolated vertex: no constraints, so the minimum value is 0.
        if not adj[s]:
            ans[s] = 0
            continue

        stack = [s]
        comp = []

        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            comp.append(u)
            bu = base[u]

            for v, z in adj[u]:
                nv = bu ^ z
                if base[v] == -1:
                    base[v] = nv
                    stack.append(v)
                elif base[v] != nv:
                    print(-1)
                    return

        cnt = [0] * K
        for u in comp:
            b = base[u]
            for k in range(K):
                if (b >> k) & 1:
                    cnt[k] += 1

        c = 0
        ncomp = len(comp)
        for k in range(K):
            # If more vertices have bit 1 than 0, flipping this bit reduces the sum.
            if cnt[k] * 2 > ncomp:
                c |= 1 << k

        for u in comp:
            ans[u] = base[u] ^ c

    print(*ans[1:])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
